Document 030: 2026-11-27_［みずほ経済インサイト］存在感を増す企業の利子収入_fb49e403

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-27_［みずほ経済インサイト］存在感を増す企業の利子収入_fb49e403.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-11-27
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

1

存在感を増す企業の利子収入 
金利上昇に伴う利子支払の負担を一部相殺

○ 金利上昇に伴い、企業には利子支払増のデメリットだけでなく、利子収入増のメリットも発生。
企業が持つ様々な資産のデータに収益率データを対応させ、先行きの利子収入を推計 
○ 2027年半ばにかけて政策金利が2.0％、長期金利が3％超に上昇すると想定した場合、企業全体の
利子収入は2026～27年度に年間1兆円超増加する見込み。利子支払増加分の3～4割を相殺 
○ 一方、2028年度は利子収入の増加ペースが急減速し、利子収支の悪化による企業の利益押し下げ
圧力が今次局面でピークに。金利上昇の影響がラグをもって企業へ影響する点に注意が必要

１．金利上昇による企業への影響には、デメリットだけでなくメリットもある 
日本銀行による利上げの進展に伴い、企業の利子支払は今後徐々に増えていくとみられる。筆者の
直近の試算では、2027年半ばにかけて日銀が政策金利を2.0％まで引き上げ、長期金利が3％をやや上
回る水準で推移すると想定した場合1、企業の借入・社債利子支払額が2026～2028年度に本格的に増加
する結果になった（服部（2026））。 
もっとも、金利上昇によって生じる企業への影響は、利子支払増加のデメリットだけではない。企
業の資産から発生する利子収入が増えるメリットもある。そこで本稿では、金利上昇による資産側の
メリットについて分析し、筆者がこれまでに分析した負債側のデメリットと比較することで、金利上
昇に伴って企業の利子収支に「いつ」「どの程度の」影響が生じるか検証する。 
マクロ経済の観点から金利上昇による企業のメリットを把握するため、本稿の分析では、日本の代
表的な企業関連統計である法人企業統計調査のデータを主に用いる（服部（2026）で分析した負債側
の試算と同様）。ただし、同調査では企業の有利子・有配当資産残高について内訳が記載されている
一方、そこから発生する資産収入のデータが「受取利息等」（預金または貸付金に対する受取利息、
有価証券利息および受取配当金の合計額）の1種類しかなく、どの種類の資産から得た収入かを区別す
ることができない2。そのため、企業がもつ様々な種類の資産に対応する収益率のデータを組み合わせ
て、積み上げ型で企業の資産収入を推計し、金利上昇によるメリットを分析する。 
２．企業の様々な有利子・有配当資産に収益率を対応させ、資産収入を推計 
企業がもつ有利子・有配当資産の一覧を示したものが、図表1だ。大きく分けて流動資産と固定資産
があり、流動資産には現金・預金と有価証券（売買目的有価証券、残存期間1年内の債券等）が、固定
資産には投資有価証券（関係会社株式、残存期間1年超の債券等）とその他（出資金、長期貸付金等）
が含まれる。特に規模が大きいのは、流動資産の現金・預金と、固定資産の株式、その他であり、2025
年度末時点残高ではこれら3項目で全体の93％を占めている。 
2026 年 11 月 27 日 
みずほ経済インサイト 
調査部 
チーフ日本経済エコノミスト

### Page 2

2

有利子・有配当資産のうち、金利変動の直接的な影響を受けるのは、預金、公社債、貸付金等の一
部資産から得られる利子収入に限られる。ただ、本稿では資産収入のベンチマークになる受取利息等
データの実績値と整合性を保つために、一旦、株式の配当も含めた資産収入全体の値を推計する。そ
の結果を踏まえたうえで、利子収入に焦点を当て、先行きの金利上昇によるメリットを分析すること
としたい。 
（１）有利子・有配当資産の各項目と収益率の対応関係 
企業が有利子・有配当資産のどの項目からどれだけ収益を得ているか推計するため、図表1の有利
子・有配当資産の各項目について、7種類の収益率データを対応させた（図表1の右端列）。 
流動資産では、現金・預金の収益率として①普通預金金利と②定期預金金利を、有価証券の収益率
として③国内企業配当率（配当金計÷純資産）3をそれぞれ用いた。なお、現金の収益率はゼロとした。
有価証券には公社債や投資信託等も含まれるが、ここでは代表的な資産として国内の株式を想定し、
保有株式残高に対する受取配当金の比率として③国内企業配当率を用いている。 
固定資産では、投資有価証券とその他について、③国内企業配当率、④国内社債金利、⑤国内銀行
貸出金利、⑥海外直接投資収益率、⑦海外証券投資収益率、の5種類の収益率が対応すると考えた4。
④国内社債金利は企業が保有する国内公社債金利の代理変数、⑤国内銀行貸出金利は企業の国内長期
貸付金金利の代理変数である。また、固定資産は海外の子会社株式や証券投資も含むと考えられるた
め、それらに⑥海外直接投資収益率、⑦海外証券投資収益率を対応させている5。 
（２）金利関連の収益率は2024 年度以降に上昇。今後一段と水準が高まる見込み 
①～⑦の収益率データがそれぞれどのように動いているか、2025年度までの実績値を確認してみよ
う（図表2）。収益率のうち金利に関連するもの（①・②・④・⑤）は、政策金利・長期金利の想定に
基づく先行きの試算値もあわせて示している。 
図表2左の①普通預金金利と②定期預金金利は、2023年度までゼロ近傍で推移してきたが、マイナス
金利が解除された2024年度から上昇し始めた。今後、2027年度半ばにかけて政策金利が2.0％まで引き
上げられると想定すると、2027～28年度には①普通預金金利が0.8％程度、②定期預金金利が1.2％程
図表 1 企業の有利子・有配当資産の一覧

模が対象。収益率との対応関係はみずほ総合研究所調査部による想定

度まで上昇すると考えられる。なお、①・②はどちらも企業以外の預金を含んでいるが、②定期預金
金利については、企業の大口預金であることを想定して預入金額1千万円以上のみを対象にした。また、
定期預金の多くは固定金利型のため、公表されている預入期間別の新規受入分金利をもとに残高全体
の平均金利を推計している。 
図表2中央の④国内社債金利（既発債平均）と⑤国内銀行貸出金利（残高全体平均）は、金利上昇に
よる企業の負債側影響を分析した服部（2026）の試算結果をそのまま使用した。預金金利と同じく2024
年度以降に上昇しているが、預金金利が2028年度以降ほぼ一定（高原状態）になるのに対し、④国内
社債金利や⑤国内銀行貸出金利は2020年代終盤になっても上昇が続く。これは、社債の平均年限や貸
出の平均期間が数年程度と長く、金利の変化が残高全体に影響するまで時間がかかるためである。 
図表2右には、金利関連以外の収益率データについて実績値のみを掲載した。③国内企業配当率はコ
ロナ禍初期の経済停滞で一旦足踏みしたものの、その後は利益率の改善に伴って上昇傾向をたどって
いる。⑥海外直接投資収益率と⑦海外証券投資収益率は、それぞれの海外資産残高に対する投資収益
の比率である（⑥は法人企業統計調査との整合性を保つため、金融・保険業を除く国内収益還流分の
みを対象にした）。⑥・⑦どちらも、海外の経済・金融市場の動向を受けて上下に大きく変動してい
ることが確認できる。 
３．2026～27 年度は利子収入が年間 1 兆円超増加。利子収支の悪化ピークは 2028 年度に 
これら①～⑦の収益率を対応する資産残高で加重平均し、有利子・有配当資産全体の資産収入につ
いて収益率を推計した（図表3）。その際、流動資産の現金・預金や固定資産の投資有価証券、その他
は、対応する収益率データが複数あるため、資金循環統計のストックデータを用いて資産額を按分し
た。なお、一部のデータの制約により、推計期間は2018～2025年度と短くなっている。 
図表3をみると、ベンチマークである受取利息等から計算した有利子・有配当資産収益率の実績値に
対し、推計値の水準がやや高いものの、両者の動きには概ね同様の上昇傾向があると言えそうだ。推
計値の水準が実績値よりコンスタントに高い一因として、投資有価証券の株式に対応させた収益率の
図表 2 企業の有利子・有配当資産に対応する収益率データの推移

利が2.0％、長期金利が3％超の水準までそれぞれ上昇すると想定した場合の試算値。③国内企業配当率、⑥海外直接投

影響が考えられる。この資産項目には大企業保有の子会社株式が多く含まれるとみられ、推計に用い
た③国内株式配当率が、子会社による親会社向け配当率の実績値を上回っている可能性があろう。 
さらに図表3では、有利子・有配当資産全体の収益率から、金利変動の直接的な影響を受ける有利子
資産の利子率のみを抜き出し、先行きも含めた推計値を示した（図中の破線）6。推計の対象とした資
産は、流動資産の普通預金と定期預金、固定資産の公社債と長期貸付金である。有利子資産利子率の
推計値は2023年度まで0.1～0.2％前後で推移していたが、2025年度には0.4％台に上昇した。今後、
2027年度には1％に達し、その後2020年代終盤にかけて1.2％程度まで上昇すると見込まれる。こうし
た有利子資産利子率の上昇が、企業にとって金利上昇のメリットになるということだ。 
では、金利上昇によって発生する企業のメリット（利子収入額の増加）とデメリット（利子支払額
の増加）を比べると、どちらがどの程度大きいか。有利子資産・負債残高がともに前年比＋2％のペー
スで増加すると簡易的に想定し、先行きの利子収入額と利子支払額の変化幅を比較したものが図表4
である。 
企業全体（除く金融・保険業）の利子支払額が2026～28年度にかけて年間約3兆円増加する一方、利
子収入額も2026年度に前年差＋1.1兆円、2027年度に同＋1.4兆円と増加し、利子支払額の増加幅の3～
4割程度を相殺する計算だ。利子収支（利子収入額－利子支払額）は2026～27年度に前年差▲1.9兆円
となり、2025年度の同▲0.9兆円から前年差マイナス幅が拡大するものの、利子収入額の増加によって
幾分軽減される。 
利子収支が前年度対比で最も悪化するのは、2028年度（前年差▲2.4兆円）である。利子支払額の増
加ペースが小幅な鈍化にとどまる一方、利子収入額の増加ペースが急減速するためだ。金利上昇によ
る直接的な減益圧力（企業利益の前年比押し下げ要因）という点では、2028年度がピークになる可能
性があろう7。 
このように、金利上昇による企業のメリット・デメリットの現れ方に時間差があるのは、資産・負
債の期間構成に違いがあり、金利上昇の反映スピードが異なることが原因だ。資産側では普通預金の

5

シェアが大きいほか、定期預金も預入期間が短いものが主流であり、金利上昇が比較的早く反映され
る。一方、負債側では社債の平均年限や銀行借入の平均期間が数年程度と長く、残高全体に金利上昇
が反映されるには時間がかかる。こうしたタイムラグがあることを十分考慮したうえで、今後、金融
環境の変化による企業への影響を見極めていくことが必要だ。

［参考文献］ 
服部直樹（2026）「金利上昇による企業の負担増加はいつ本格化するか」 みずほ経済インサイト 2026
年10月15日 
みずほ総合研究所調査部（2026）「 2026年・2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ～転換点は近いのか、投資
主導経済の行方を探る～」 2026年10月2日

1 みずほ総合研究所調査部（2026）の見通しに基づく想定。 
2 法人企業統計調査には年次別調査と四半期別調査の 2 種類があり、調査対象の範囲や項目が一部異なる。本稿では対象企業が
多い年次別調査を原則として用いているが、受取利息等のデータは四半期別調査でしか取得できないことから、四半期別調査
のデータを基に年次別調査ベースで受取利息等を再推計している。 
3 企業の配当金には中間配当と期末配当があるが、一般的に期末配当は前年度分を当年度に受け取ると考えられることから、③
国内企業配当率は当年度と前年度の値を当年度中間配当額と前年度期末配当額で加重平均した値を用いている。 
4 固定資産のその他には残存期間 1 年超の定期預金が含まれるが、日本銀行「定期預金の残高および新規受入高」（預入金額 1
千万円以上）によれば預入期間 2 年以上の残高シェアは 2025 年度末時点で 13.8％と小さく、定期預金の大半は流動資産に分
類されると考えられることから、固定資産には定期預金金利を収益率として対応させていない。 
5 厳密には、流動資産の預金に海外資産（外貨預金）が含まれうるほか、流動資産の有価証券にも海外証券投資が含まれうる
が、どちらも規模が小さいと考えられるため本稿の推計では考慮していない。なお、法人企業統計調査では各資産が国内資産
か海外資産か区別していないため、図表 1 では資産項目と収益率が一対一で対応していない。 
6 金利上昇は、企業利益や株主還元圧力等の変化を通じて国内企業配当率にも影響しうるが、本稿では考慮していない。 
7 金利上昇の影響にはこの他にも、設備投資や耐久財消費等の需要抑制や、為替レートの変化を通じた間接的な影響が考えられ
るが、本稿では利子収支の変化による直接的な影響のみ推計している。

AI 資金壓力拖累科技股，惟產業需求仍具韌性
2026 年 10 月 23 日
一、 市場動態
1. 費半再度重挫，科技股成為美股主要賣壓來源：美國股市主要指數 10 月 22 日跌多漲
少，道瓊工業指數上漲 0.02%，收 52,867.98 點；S&P 500 指數下跌 1.21%，收 7,663.43
點；Nasdaq 綜合指數下跌 2.60%，收 26,008.86 點，創 6 月 5 日以來最大單日跌幅
。費城半導體指數重挫 6.72%，收 10,731.98 點，跌幅明顯大於主要股價指數，顯示
AI 及半導體族群仍是本波市場修正的主要壓力來源。

2. AI 相關企業全面承壓，資金流向非科技股及公債：10 月 22 日 Meta 下跌約 7%、Lam
Research 跌幅逾一成，Nvidia 下跌 4.4%，其他 AI 相關股票亦明顯走低，顯示市場
對 AI 資本支出及投資報酬的疑慮已由大型科技平台延伸至半導體與基礎建設供應商
。不過，道瓊上漲 0.02%，羅素 2000 指數亦小幅收紅，反映資金並未全面撤出股市
；美國 10 年期公債殖利率則因避險需求跌破 5%，顯示資金由高評價科技股轉向其他
產業及避險資產。
3. 中東地緣政治風險升溫，油價攀至 5 月以來高點：中東能源運輸及供給風險 10 月 22
日再度升高，布蘭特原油價格上漲約 3.76%，升至每桶 113 美元附近，創 5 月以來高
點。油價攀升除使市場重新關注能源成本對通膨與 Fed 政策路徑的影響，也可能增加
AI 資料中心的電力及營運成本。中東情勢雖非本次半導體股重挫的唯一因素，但在市
場已擔憂 AI 投資效益的情況下，能源價格上揚進一步壓抑投資人風險偏好。
二、 下跌原因分析：

1. 殖利率回落難抵科技股下跌，顯示修正因素出現轉變：10 月 14 日費半重挫主要受到
美國 9 月 CPI 高於預期、升息疑慮及美債殖利率盤中急升影響，惟當日 10 年期殖利
率收盤反而小幅下降；10 月 22 日情況則更加明顯，美國 10 年期公債殖利率因避險
需求盤中跌破 5%，費半仍大跌 6.72%。這顯示市場關注焦點已由單純的利率與估值
壓力，逐步轉向 AI 資本支出的資金來源、融資能力及投資回收期。因此，即使後續殖
利率回落，若企業仍無法證明 AI 投資能轉化為足夠營收與現金流，科技股評價仍可能
持續受到壓抑。
2. Meta 自由現金流轉負，AI 投資成本由預期轉為實際財務壓力：Meta 第三季營收 646.0
億美元、年增 26%，顯示核心業務仍維持強勁成長；但資本支出達 349.1 億美元，超
過同期營業現金流 327.9 億美元，使自由現金流由去年同期正 106.3 億美元轉為負
21.2 億美元。公司亦將全年資本支出預估調整至 1,350～1,450 億美元，並宣布設立

無表決權 Class C 股票，增加未來資本運用彈性。這反映 AI 投資規模已開始超越部分

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
大型科技企業當期現金創造能力，但 Meta 仍持有 824.5 億美元現金、約當現金及有
價證券，不能將單季自由現金流轉負直接解讀為財務危機。
3. 資本稀缺逐漸成為 AI 擴張瓶頸，新興雲端業者面臨更大壓力：AI 發展需要持續投入
GPU、伺服器、資料中心及電力基礎建設，龐大資金需求使融資能力逐漸成為決定企
業成長速度的重要因素。相較具備穩定營業現金流與龐大資產規模的大型科技平台，
新興 AI 雲端服務商如 CoreWeave 及 Nebius 高度依賴外部融資，因而更容易受到借
貸成本、信用利差及投資人風險偏好變化影響。我們認為，這次修正並不代表 AI 運算

需求突然消失，而是市場開始重新評估不同企業能否負擔下一階段的擴張成本。
4. 半導體設備財報強勁卻遭賣壓，市場提高獲利兌現標準：Lam Research 於 10 月 21
日公布截至 2026 年 9 月季度（FY2027 Q1）營收 82.19 億美元，年增 54.4%、季增
22%，非 GAAP 營業利益率達 40.2%，下一季營收指引為 86 億美元、上下浮動 4 億
美元。然而，即使營運仍快速成長，10 月 22 日股價仍大幅下跌。我們研判，市場已
由過去重視 AI 訂單與營收成長，轉向同時檢視未來成長能否超越原先偏高預期，以及
AI 投資是否具備足夠的財務報酬。當股價已反映高度成長期待，即使財報表現良好，
也可能因評價調整與籌碼降溫而承壓。
5. 油價再度上漲，Fed 政策風險仍可能限制科技股評價：儘管 10 月 22 日美債殖利率因
避險需求下滑，但能源價格上漲仍可能延緩通膨降溫，使 Fed 後續政策路徑維持不確
定性。若油價持續高檔，不僅可能推升企業運輸、電力及營運成本，也將增加通膨預

期，進而限制長天期美債殖利率的下行空間。因此，科技股短線同時面臨 AI 投資報酬
受到重新檢視，以及能源價格與利率政策不確定的雙重壓力。後續 10 月 28 日 FOMC
會後聲明及主席記者會，將是評估利率環境的重要依據。
三、 市場看法：
1. 股市下跌但多數個股上漲，顯示賣壓集中於大型科技股：10 月 22 日 S&P 500 指數下
跌 1.21%，但根據美聯社（AP）報導，約三分之二成分股逆勢上漲，追蹤 S&P 500
等權重指數的 ETF（RSP）更上漲 0.73%，道瓊工業指數亦小幅收紅，呈現市值加權
指數與市場廣度明顯背離的現象。這反映本波修正主要集中於權重較高的 AI 及大型科
技股，而非整體美股同步走弱。相較上週以防禦性類股輪動為主的情況，本週更值得
注意的是市場對高資本支出及高評價科技企業的重新定價，資金仍持續流向其他產業
，而非全面性風險撤離。只要賣壓未進一步擴散，現階段仍較接近科技股評價調整與
類股輪動，而非整體美股多頭格局反轉。
2. 台積電財測再度上修，顯示 AI 需求尚未出現結構性反轉：台積電 10 月 15 日法說會

將 2026 年美元營收成長預估由原先略高於 40%上修至約 44%，第三季營收達新台幣
1.49 兆元、年增 50.9%，稅後純益年增 68.7%，每股盈餘 29.43 元、同樣年增 68.7%
；第四季美元營收預估為 523～543 億美元，以中間值計算季增約 14%。此外，Lam
Research 截至 9 月季度營收年增 54.4%，亦顯示半導體設備需求仍強勁。前述這些

### Page 3

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
數據與近期科技股重挫形成明顯對比，反映目前市場主要擔憂 AI 投資回收及股票評價
，而非半導體終端需求同步惡化。只要大型雲端服務商未全面縮減 AI 資本支出，先進
製程與設備需求中期仍具支撐。
3. AI 投資進入報酬驗證階段，營收成長能否追上資本投入成為關鍵：我們認為，AI 產業
目前正由過去的資本支出擴張階段，逐步轉向投資報酬率與自由現金流的驗證階段。
以 Meta 為例，第三季營收仍成長 26%，但資本支出已超過營業現金流，顯示即使
AI 技術有助於改善廣告推薦及營收變現，龐大基礎建設投入仍可能使短期自由現金流

承壓。不過，資料中心及運算設備通常具有多年使用年限，不能僅以單季現金流評估
整體投資成敗。真正關鍵在於未來 AI 服務收入、營業利益及現金流成長，能否逐步抵
銷前期資本投入。具備明確商業化模式、較高資本效率及穩定現金流的企業，後續表
現可望優於高度依賴融資與成長預期的業者。
4. AI 融資風險仍集中於部分企業，尚不足以認定信用危機全面擴散：Meta Q3 財報顯示
，該公司持有 824.5 億美元現金、約當現金及有價證券，顯示其具備一定財務緩衝能
力；相較之下，新興 AI 雲端業者因資料中心建置成本龐大、現金流尚未成熟，對信用
市場與外部融資條件的敏感度相對較高。雖然 AI 資本支出引發投資人重新評估企業信
用風險，但我們認為目前仍缺乏足夠證據認定風險已擴散至整體投資級債市場或銀行
體系。然而，後續仍宜密切觀察 AI 相關企業 CDS 與公司債利差變化、再融資能力及
主要金融機構授信條件對於科技類股及整體股債市場的後續影響。
5. 股價修正不代表企業獲利同步下修，財報表現仍有機會支撐科技股：儘管近期半導體

及 AI 相關股價回落，但台積電與 Lam Research 最新財報顯示，部分供應鏈企業營收
及獲利仍維持高度成長，反映股票價格與企業基本面短期可能出現明顯落差。當市場
因融資及資本支出疑慮而降低評價倍數時，即使企業獲利持續增加，股價也可能暫時
承壓。然而，若後續大型科技公司公布的雲端營收、AI 訂單與獲利展望持續優於預期
，企業 EPS 上修仍有機會抵銷部分本益比壓縮壓力。因此，目前尚不宜將科技股價格
修正直接等同於 AI 產業景氣反轉，企業獲利能否持續兌現，仍是中期市場方向的重要
支撐。

### Page 4

6. Fed 與大型科技股財報進入驗證期，短線震盪可能延續：Fed 將於 10 月 27～28 日舉
行利率決策會議，市場除關注是否調整政策利率外，更應重視會後聲明及主席記者會
對通膨、油價與後續利率路徑的態度。另一方面，大型雲端服務商後續公布的財報，
將提供 AI 資本支出、雲端營收及投資效率的最新證據。短線科技股仍可能受到市場評
價偏高、融資風險及地緣政治因素影響而維持震盪；不過，只要企業獲利成長與 AI
需求未出現結構性反轉，中期科技產業基本面仍具支撐，現階段維持短線審慎、中期
偏正向的看法。
四、市場展望：

1. 科技股短線宜審慎觀察，中期仍看好具基本面支撐的 AI 產業：近期科技股受到 AI 資本支
出、融資能力與評價調整等因素影響，短線波動可能延續，尤其高度依賴外部資金及獲利
尚未成熟的 AI 基礎建設業者，仍面臨較大不確定性。然而，台積電及 Lam Research 最
新財報顯示，先進製程與半導體設備需求尚未出現全面轉弱。我們認為，科技股中期展望
仍偏正向，但市場將更重視企業營收成長、訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率。相較缺
乏穩定獲利的 AI 概念企業，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健及獲利成長能見度的科技企業，
風險報酬相對有利。
2. AI 產業由全面成長轉向基本面分化，現金流將成為選股關鍵：隨著大型科技企業持續擴
大 AI 基礎建設投資，市場對未來投資報酬率的要求將同步提高。就目前已公布的科技股
財報結果來看，AI 產業長期成長趨勢尚未改變，但後續股價表現將更加分化，具備穩定
現金流、明確訂單及商業化能力的企業，相對有利；反之，對於高度依賴外部融資、自由
現金流持續為負且缺乏明確獲利時程的企業，仍宜維持審慎。

3. 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：半導體類股 10/22 重挫，美國公債
價格上揚、10 年期殖利率盤中跌破 5%，顯示股市風險偏好下降時，美債仍可提供一定
避險效果。雖然 Fed 政策路徑及能源價格仍存在不確定性，但預期相較受政策利率影響
較大的短天期公債，以及受財政赤字、國債供給與期限溢價影響的超長天期公債，中天期
美國公債的風險報酬仍相對較為均衡。投資級債因企業信用基本面整體仍具韌性，中期維
持偏正向看法；高收益債則須留意 AI 相關發行人信用風險上升及利差擴大的影響。

多重利空夾擊，全球 AI 股連日重挫
2026 年 11 月 20 日
一、市場動態
1. 美股 11/19 反彈遭逢賣壓：Anthropic 19 日於那斯達克掛牌，募資約 813 億美元創史
上最大 IPO，開盤大漲 34%、盤中市值一度突破 2 兆美元，終場漲幅收斂至 17.1%，
然激勵效果未能擴散至其他 AI 股，反被視為「利多出盡」的獲利了結訊號。美股四大
指數開高走低、全數重挫逾 2%：道瓊下跌 2.19%，為 6 月以來首度跌破 50,000 點；
標普 500 跌 2.02%、連六黑，逾 460 檔成分股收黑；那斯達克跌 2.25%、費半跌 2.41%，

VIX 盤中一度升近 29、創 3 月以來新高。個股方面，輝達早盤一度上漲 4.7%後翻黑，
收跌 3.3%，本月累計跌幅約 28%；AMD 遭放空機構 Hunterbrook 指控與受美國制裁
的中國伺服器業者往來，重挫 7.72%，高度依賴舉債擴張的 CoreWeave、Nebius 分

場預估 12 月升息機率約 96%；初領失業金人數亦高於預期，增添景氣疑慮。
2. 亞股 11/20 續挫，台股同步承壓：亞股 11/20 早盤延續美股走勢，賣壓擴及亞洲 AI
硬體供應鏈，日經 225 與韓國 KOSPI 同步重挫逾 4%。台股 19 日下跌 1,768.02 點，
收 38,426.89 點，跌幅 4.40%，創史上收盤第 7 大跌點後，20 日再受美股下挫拖累，
加權指數持續走低，截至台灣時間下午 1 點 22 分為止，台股加權指數下挫 6.80%；台
積電、聯發科、台達電等權值股同步重挫。

安全局（BIS）於 11 月 16 日發布臨時最終規則、並於 19 日生效，將管制由實體晶片

擴大至「提供雲端算力給中國客戶的第三國資料中心」，並將印尼、馬來西亞、新加坡、
泰國及巴西列為高轉運風險國家，同時限縮晶圓代工與封測廠的許可豁免。此次新規
由財政部與白宮國安會主導起草，反映黃仁勳辭任後政策方向明顯從嚴。對台股而言，

4. Anthropic 史上最大 IPO 引發資金排擠與「利多出盡」情緒：Anthropic 募資約 813
億美元，盤中市值一度突破 2 兆美元，但美股開高走低、晶片股由紅翻黑，市場將其視
為 AI 交易階段性高點的獲利了結訊號。市場亦解讀，大型 AI 模型公司直接上市，讓資
金可直接押注 AI 應用端，相對降低透過硬體供應鏈間接參與的需求，並在短期內吸納
部分 AI 配置資金。
5. 台股籌碼面賣壓與融資斷頭形成負向循環：外資 11 月累計賣超達 8,303 億元，台指期
淨空單維持約 9.9 萬口；台股跌破年線後融資維持率偏低，斷頭與停損賣壓同步出籠，
新台幣亦貶至 33 元附近，加重外資匯損考量。金管會雖自 20 日起實施限空令 2.0（個

股收跌逾 3.5%者隔日禁止平盤下放空、盤中借券賣出上限由 3%降至 1%），但短線仍
難完全抵銷被動賣壓。
資料來源：臺灣證券交易所、元大銀行整理
三、市場看法
1. AI 需求尚未反轉，但產業正由資本支出驅動轉向獲利驗證：我們認為，判斷 AI 產業是
否出現結構性反轉，關鍵應在於終端運算需求、CSP 資本支出及供應鏈訂單是否持續下

### Page 4

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
降，而非單純觀察股價跌幅。輝達第三季資料中心營收年增 110%，且 Vera Rubin 平
台已進入出貨階段，顯示先進運算設備需求仍具有實質支撐。此外，Reuters 引述 LSEG
資料指出，輝達財報公布後，分析師對下一財年調整後淨利的平均預估由 3,800 億美元
上修至 3,930 億美元，但股價評價反而轉趨保守。這項變化反映市場並未全面否定輝達
的獲利能力，而是開始要求更高的風險補償。我們認為，若未來 CSP 未大幅縮減資本
支出，AI 晶片與相關供應鏈仍具有中長期成長基礎；但若資本支出成長持續快於營收

及現金流改善，科技股評價仍可能承壓。
2. AI 信用風險仍較集中於高槓桿業者，尚未顯示全面性信用危機：目前市場對 AI 企業融
資風險的擔憂逐漸升高，但不同企業的財務承受能力存在明顯差異。大型 CSP 通常擁
有成熟的雲端、廣告或軟體業務，可透過營業現金流支應部分 AI 投資；相較之下，部
分新興資料中心業者高度依賴債務、設備融資或外部股權資金，對利率與資本市場環境
更加敏感。10 月 FOMC 會議紀錄指出，投資級公司債利差僅小幅擴大，且以歷史標準
衡量仍相對偏低；高收益債利差則擴大較明顯，尤其與資料中心融資有關的債券受到更
大衝擊。這顯示市場正在提高部分高風險企業的融資門檻，但尚不足以判定信用壓力已
全面擴散至整體投資級債市場。我們認為，後續仍應觀察相關企業的債務到期結構、再
融資需求與信用利差變化，判斷風險是否進一步擴大。
3. 科技股次產業將進一步分化：隨著市場由追逐 AI 投資規模轉向評估投資效益，科技次
產業未來表現可能明顯分歧。晶圓代工與先進封裝產業具有較高技術門檻，仍可受惠先

進運算晶片需求；HBM 及高階記憶體則具備 AI 伺服器成長題材，但其獲利仍需觀察供
需循環及價格變化；半導體設備業者則較容易受到客戶建廠時程及資本支出調整影響。
另一方面，部分尚未建立穩定營收模式的 AI 基礎建設業者，可能因融資成本與投資回
收壓力，面臨更大的評價修正。我們認為，科技股中期並非全面轉空，而是市場可能更
偏好具備技術競爭優勢、穩定訂單、獲利成長及自由現金流的企業。未來即使 AI 產業
整體維持成長，也不代表所有相關公司的營收、獲利及股價都能同步受惠。
4. Fed 政策仍有不確定性，但企業獲利成長可望部分抵銷評價壓力：目前 Fed 升息預期
已對全球科技股評價形成明顯壓力，但未來市場方向不僅取決於 12 月是否升息，更重
要的是升息幅度與後續政策路徑是否超出市場預期。然而，美國經濟與企業獲利目前尚
未出現全面性衰退跡象，若未來企業營收與獲利持續成長，即使本益比難以回到過去高
檔，仍有助於股市透過基本面改善逐步修復跌幅。我們認為，Fed 政策風險不容忽視，
但也不宜單憑升息預期就認定股市中長期多頭結構已全面改變。

5. 台股短線仍需消化籌碼壓力，但企業獲利成長仍可提供中期支撐：若以台股走勢來看，
其近期跌幅明顯超過部分國際市場，我們認為除受到美股科技股修正影響，也與大型電
子權值股集中、外資調節及短線市場流動性下降有關。不過，根據金管會公布數據，截
至 2026 年 10 月底，國內上市櫃公司累計營收約 56.75 兆元，年增 39.05%；前三季
累計稅前淨利約 6.61 兆元，年增 86.43%，顯示整體企業獲利仍具成長支撐。此外，
金管會自 11 月 20 日起擴大限空措施，對收盤跌幅逾 3.5%的個股實施次日平盤下放空
限制，並將盤中借券賣出數量上限由 3%降至 1%。我們認為，政策措施有助降低部分

短線賣壓，但市場真正止穩仍需觀察外資流向、融資去槓桿是否趨緩，以及主要電子權
值股的股價與基本面是否恢復穩定。
6. 後續觀察重點：（1）美國下週公布核心 PCE 物價指數，市場預期年增 3.2%，將影響
12 月升息幅度；（2）12 月 FOMC 升息 1 碼或 2 碼，以及 2027 年升息路徑；（3）輝
達新任執行長人選與黃仁勳赴參議院作證情況，牽動出口管制後續走向；（4）BIS 新規
執行細節及東南亞資料中心許可審查進度。
四、 投資建議
1. 短線嚴控槓桿與部位集中度，選股邏輯轉向「現金流與訂單能見度」優先：我們認為，

AI 產業成長動能尚未確認反轉，但市場已不再接受單純依靠資本支出擴張支撐評價。
前述利空因素短期內恐難完全消除或明朗，市場短線仍將隨著消息面的發展而變化。我
們預期後續投資人將更重視科技股的訂單能見度、企業獲利、資產負債表及自由現金
流。同時，台灣在先進製程、先進封裝及 AI 伺服器領域仍具競爭優勢，中期基本面展
望維持偏正向。然而，近期指數連續重挫，反映市場流動性與籌碼壓力尚未解除，短期
仍須觀察外資賣壓、融資水位及美股科技股走勢。投資人宜採取分批及分散方式參與中
長期產業成長，同時避免過度集中於融資需求高、獲利能見度較低的 AI 企業。
2. 以股債平衡穿越波動，固定收益「先短後長」：Fed 仍處升息循環，長天期債券價格仍
有波動風險，但美國公債目前具有高殖利率吸引力與防禦價值，建議以中短天期美國公
債、高信評投資等級債及美元貨幣型商品鎖定相對高檔的收益，待升息循環接近尾聲再
逐步拉長存續期間。投資級債則受惠相對穩健的信用品質，惟仍須關注 AI 相關企業信
用風險是否進一步擴散。

Hon Precision
Valuation
Our target price of NT$10,000 is based on a target PER multiple of 31x applied to the average of our 2027E/28E EPS estimates.
Our PER target of 31x is set at 0.5-std above its 1-year PER average, justified by its high share of the AI/HPC testing handler
market, which we estimate could support an earnings CAGR of >75% in 2025-28, higher than its 3-year CAGR at c40% in

2022-25. Our estimated >75% earning CAGR is also higher than the +40% average (Bloomberg consensus) of its global testing
equipment peers. As its peers have been trading at  average  35x PER on 2027/28 consensus EPS in the past 1 year, we believe
Hon Precision deserves a further re-rating to our target PER.
Risks
Our quantitative risk rating system, which looks at historical share price volatility, rates Hon Precision High Risk. However, we
are not assigning a High Risk rating as we forecast an earnings CAGR of >75%, significantly stronger than the growth outlook

for its global peers. Also, we note its dominant position in AI/HPC handler market and expanding market TAM opportunity,
which support our constructive view over its growth outlook.
Key downside risks that could impede the shares from reaching our target price: 1) Global AI demand slowdown  driving weaker
capacity build for semiconductor equipment; 2) unexpected share losses at its major AI projects; 3) slower-than-expected
capacity ramp at its new facilities, or the company taking a long time to find/secure new sites; 4) slower-than-expected

development of its CPO business; 5) shorter-than-expected testing time leading to lower demand for its products; 6) weak
adoption of high-premium cold plate products; and 7) increasing competition from new entrants.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

受惠電動工具與智能傳動需求暢旺，鈞興 26Q3 單季 EPS 創歷史
次高，達 2.98 元。營收創歷史新高 9.38 億元，QoQ 28.94%、YoY 
16.27%，毛利率 33.48%，季減 0.76 個百分點、年減 0.74 個百分
點，營業利益 1.86 億元，QoQ 67.18%、YoY 16.80%，稅後淨利
1.56 億元，QoQ 70.41%、YoY 17.18%。 
展望後市，電動工具新案出貨順暢，訂單能見度已達 2027 年 1 月，
預期 26Q4 營收 8.55 億元，QoQ -8.82%、YoY 17.07%，營業利
益 1.70 億元，QoQ -8.47%、YoY 25.64%，EPS 2.68 元，帶動全
年營收創高。 
2027 年電動工具受惠美國基建、製造業回流與客戶新品放量，預
估維持個位數年增，園林工具則視美國利率與房市動向而定。高毛
利智能傳動工業機器人齒輪訂單能見度 3 至 6 個月，2026 年佔比
站穩 10%以上，2027 年營收延續雙位數成長並優化產品組合；人
形機器人已向中台美客戶送樣，預期 2027 年部分客戶進入小量量
產。產能方面，越南二期廠房預計 2026 年底完工、2027 年投產，
強化非中供應鏈布局。 
研究部預估 26/27 年營收 33.05/35.24 億元，YoY 7.83%/6.63%，
毛利率 33.92%/34.11%，年增 1.15/0.19 個百分點，營業利益
6.16/7.32 億元，YoY 15.76%/18.89%，稅後淨利 5.14/5.97 億元，
YoY 3.16%/16.19%，EPS 為 9.82 元/11.26 元。 
評價方面，公司過往 5 年 PER 評價集中於 15x-20x，考量後市客
戶新品上市將帶動營收成長，且產品組合優化能持續挹注獲利表
現，故給予歷史本益比區間中緣偏上之 17x PER 評價，目標價 
190 元，投資建議為買進。

人形機器人已向中台美廠商送樣 
智能傳動與機器人業務，除穩定供應工業焊接、搬運機械手臂

場需求。

續，持續帶動營收成長，預估 26/27 年營收為 33.05 億元/35.24
億元，YoY 7.83%/6.63%；獲利方面，受惠智能傳動營收規模

9.82 元/11.26 元。

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
악조건 속에서도 이 정도 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 영업실적은 매출액 5조 7,536억원(yoy +30.2%, qoq 
`-9.1%), 영업이익 8,992억원(yoy +61.3%, qoq -13.5%)으로 당사 추정치에 대체로 
부합하였다. 조업일수 감소와 원화 강세로 매출액이 감소했으며, 임단협 타결로 
인한 성과금 및 격려금이 당분기에 비용으로 반영되었다. 해양플랜트 부문에서는 
RUYA 프로젝트의 공사 진행률 확대와 TRION 프로젝트의 C/O(계약 변경) 
180억원이 추가로 반영되며 매출액이 전분기대비 0.7% 증가하였다. 엔진기계 
부문은 환율 하락을 판가 상승과 D/F(이중연료) 비중 증가로 상쇄하며 전분기와 
유사한 영업실적을 달성했다. 
2027년에도 지속될 수주 시점 효과 
3분기 대형선 야드의 수주 시점별 매출 비중은 2022년 4%, 2023년 45%, 2024년 
31%, 2025년 20% 수준으로 파악된다. 상대적으로 선가가 낮았던 2022~2023년 
물량이 여전히 매출의 절반 가량을 차지하고 있는 셈이다. 따라서 2027년 말까지도 
고선가 물량의 매출 비중 확대가 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이는 LNG운반선 매출 
비중 감소에도 불구하고 수익성을 방어할 수 있는 핵심 요인으로 작용할 전망이다.   
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 550,000원으로 하향 
투자의견은 매수로 유지하나, 목표주가는 550,000원으로 34.5% 하향한다. 환율 
가정을 기존 1,450원 수준에서 1,400원으로 조정함에 따라 목표주가 산정의 기준이 
되는 2027~2028년 실적추정치를 하향조정하였다. 또한 미국 네오 클라우드 
업체들의 자금 조달 여건 악화로 데이터센터 증설이 지연되고 있어, 발전용 엔진 
증설을 근거로 동사에 부여하였던 프리미엄을 제거하여 P/E 배수를 19.8배로 
하향조정했다. 그러나 본업인 상선 부문은 향후 고부가가치 가스선의 발주 강세를 
전망하기 때문에 투자의견은 매수로 유지한다.

동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15

Status: ok

### Page 1

HD현대중공업의 3Q26 연결 실적은 매출액 5조 8,602억원(yoy +33%, qoq -7%), 
영업이익 9,161억원(yoy +64%, qoq -12%, OPM 15.6%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 하회할 
전망이다. 동사의 3Q26 조업일수는 여름휴가, 추석연휴 및 중대재해 등 영향으로 
qoq -21% 감소했으며 환율 역시 qoq -84원 감소한 영향을 감안해 전분기 대비 
실적을 보수적으로 추정했다. 그럼에도 해양 부문 Trion FPU발 C/O에 따른 일회성 
환입이 예상됨에 따라 일부 이익 상쇄가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 한편, 동사의 
임단협 부결로 당분기 추가적인 인건비 부담은 제한적일 것으로 판단되며 잠수함 
화재 사고 관련 비용 역시 4Q 이후 반영될 전망이다.

동사는 급격히 커져가는 AIDC향 전력수요 및 발전용 엔진 수주에 대응하고자 9/10 
총 4GW 규모의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 증설을 위한 신규 투자를 공시헸다. 동사의 
기존 DC향 4행정 중속엔진 수주 단가를 고려 시 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 
가능할 전망이며 2행정 및 선박용 4행정 엔진 대비 높은 수익성이 예상되는 바 
생산이 본격화될 28년부터 동사 실적 성장성의 핵심 동인으로 작용할 전망이다.

동사에 대해 투자의견 BUY를 유지하며 업종 밸류에이션 디레이팅 및 시장 프리미엄 
조정에 따른 Target Multipe 변동으로 목표주가를 70만원으로 하향 조정한다. 상선 
시장 수주 경쟁 심화로 업종 밸류 조정이 이루어지고 있으나 동사는 엔진 기반 장기 
성장성으로 Peer 대비 상대적 매력도 확보 및 주가 방어가 가능하다는 판단이다.

Document 256: AI is boosting business creations both in the US and Europe (2026-11-04)

### Page 1

4 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AI IS BOOSTING BUSINESS CREATIONS BOTH IN THE US AND EUROPE
Stéphane Colliac
The significant increase in business creations since mid-2025 in the United States, France and Germany – and, to a lesser extent, in the United
Kingdom – is continuing. Across the board, this trend is being driven by the information and communication sector and mostly by AI. It is
spreading to other business services, while developments in consumer services, industry and construction are more mixed. However, in both

the United States and France, the trend is broadening, suggesting that AI is facilitating business creation across all sectors.
Business creations by sector: a boom that is building momentum and spreading

We have calculated the year-on-year change in the number of new business start-ups. The table categorises these results as positive (red), neutral (white) or negative (blue). The darker
the colour, the larger the change. The data series are defined as follows: Germany = new entries in the companies register (start-ups) since 2008; France = business start-ups, including

micro-entrepreneurs, since 2000; United Kingdom = businesses registered for VAT/PAYE since 2017; United States = business applications (tax identification number) since 2004. Sectors
have been grouped together to ensure that the classification is standardised across all countries. For the third quarter, the German data only include the months of July and August.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 4 November 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A noticeable boom in the United States, France, and Germany, but not quite as pronounced in the United Kingdom
The four economies for which we have comparable data (the United States, France, Germany and the United Kingdom) have all
seen, to varying degrees, an increase in the number of new business creations. While the timing varies across these countries,

most of them are enjoying strong momentum. In the United States, there has been a noticeable acceleration since the second
quarter of 2025, which has continued to strengthen. Germany has followed a similar trajectory, moving from slight declines at
the start of 2024 to sharp increases. France is experiencing the same trend but with some specific features, as momentum has
picked up significantly after months of slowdown (due to the uncertainty following the dissolution of the National Assembly) and is

accelerating across most sectors in 2026. In the United Kingdom, the trend is less pronounced and the increase may not last.
Business services, and AI in particular, are driving this momentum
In most of these four countries, business creations are rising significantly in the information and communication sector, which is
directly linked to AI. In the United States, Germany and France, there has been a pronounced trend which is gaining momentum.
In the United Kingdom, the AI sector is also seeing an increase, but it is significantly less pronounced. However, this momentum is

not limited to this sector. It is spreading to many services sectors, including finance, real estate and business services. Growth has
been sustained since mid-2025 in the United States and Germany, and since the third quarter of 2025 in France.
The picture for consumer services is more mixed. In the United States, trade, accommodation and catering have experienced major
increases in creations since mid-2025. In France, these sectors are in positive territory, particularly trade, where the increase in

business creations has recently accelerated. In Germany, these same sectors fell into negative territory in 2026 following a rather
mixed year in 2025.
The situation is even more mixed for manufacturing and construction. In the United States, these sectors are also part of the
overall positive momentum in the third quarter of 2026. This shows that AI does not only benefit sectors closely linked to it
(notably the digital technology sector) but that it makes it easier to start a business across the entire economy (by simplifying

administrative procedures in particular). In France, the upturn in business creations in the construction sector suggests that this
same trend is at play, even as activity in the sector continues to contract.
Therefore, the trend is more widespread in the United States and is becoming increasingly prevalent in France. It is driven by
specific sectors of the services industry in Germany, while it is significantly more subdued in the United Kingdom.

stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

Document 259: AI估值被严重误判！贝莱德基金经理驳斥“见顶论”：Anthropic上市标志着应用层大爆发

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI估值被严重误判！贝莱德基金经理驳斥“见顶论”：Anthropic上市标志着应用层大爆发.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-26
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

⻉莱德AI主题ETF基⾦经理Tony Kim认为，市场严重误判了AI估值：芯⽚硬件股盈利⾼增
却已跌⾄低于标普500的⽔平，背后是"2027年⻅顶、2028年下滑"的⼀致预期。模型能⼒
每年提升⼗倍、落地速度远未跟上，AI交易正从算⼒层切向应⽤层，Anthropic融资事件是
分⽔岭；五年后机器流量将占互联⽹99%，Agent⾝份安全与数据层是最先受益的环节。
摘要：
管理着约140亿美元⻉莱德AI主题ETF的Tony Kim，对当下华尔街最流⾏的⼀个判断给出了截然
相反的答案。
他在彭博《Trillions》播客中认为，市场正在为"AI资本开⽀2027年⻅顶、2028年⾛平甚⾄下
滑"定价，直接后果是——芯⽚与硬件公司的盈利在成倍增⻓，股价却在跌，估值已经低于标普
500整体⽔平。"我确实认为市场在向下超调。"
在他看来，这不是估值分歧，⽽是对"变化速率"的系统性低估：模型能⼒每年提升约⼀个数量
级，⽽现实经济的采⽤速度远远落后于这条曲线。
他把Anthropic的⼀级市场融资事件视为分⽔岭——AI交易正从"建算⼒"切换到"跑应⽤"。

芯⽚股⽐⼤盘还便宜，市场在给⼀个"2027⻅顶"的剧本定价
Kim 的第⼀层论证从估值结构切⼊。他把当下的科技板块拆成两块：芯⽚与硬件，以及超⼤
⼚。
原因不在基本⾯，在预期。
他把这称作当前最⼤的分歧点："要么你相信AI会继续下去，那这些东西便宜得离谱；要么你相
信AI会⻅顶回落，那市场的恐惧就是合理的。"
超⼤⼚同样被下杀了。Kim 认为，⾕歌、微软、Meta 这类公司正在把业务的根本重⼼转向算⼒
基础设施提供⽅，"它们把每⼀块钱利润都投进资本开⽀，⾃由现⾦流因此掉下来甚⾄转负，估
值⾃然被打下来"。他的判断是这类公司的资本开⽀还要再烧"两三年"，之后才会迎来回报。
⾄于市场为何在此时杀估值，他归因于融资能⼒与投资回报两个担忧叠加。今年约⼀万亿美元
的资本开⽀中，"85%到90%来⾃最⼤的那⼏家公司，只有10%到15%落在那些资产负债表不够
厚的第⼆梯队新云⼚商⾝上"，⽽这部分恰恰是市场最担⼼的环节，英伟达还是其中最⼤的⽀持
者之⼀。
"这些公司现在的估值⽐标普500还便宜。我知道这听起来很离谱。它们的盈利在成倍增⻓，股

价却在往下⾛，所以市盈率越压越低。"
"市场认为AI会在2027年⻅顶。⽽且⼀种越来越成形的看法是，2028年会⾛平甚⾄下滑。"
"我认为市场对这⻔⽣意的融资能⼒做了过度修正。"
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⼗倍年增速与落地速度之间的裂缝
⽀撑 Kim 逆向判断的核⼼，是⼀个被他反复强调的概念：变化的速率。
他提到，随着"递归⾃我改进"这类机制出现，这条曲线的斜率还可能更陡。但真正的⽭盾在于另
⼀端：能⼒在⻜跃，采⽤在爬⾏。
这条裂缝正是他解释"看不到ROI"质疑的钥匙："⼤家说这项投资看不到回报，我认为它最终会显
现出来，只是也许不是现在，是明年、后年。"
同样的逻辑也解释了为什么他期待应⽤层。Kim 把AI拆成两层：⼀层是"原⼦"——电⼒、芯
⽚、服务器、云、超⼤⼚构成的计算与基础设施层；另⼀层是"电⼦"——软件与服务构成的应⽤
层。过去四年，全部故事都发⽣在第⼀层。
Agent 数量将百倍于⼈⼝，安全与数据是最先被点燃的环节
Kim 对应⽤层最先落地的⽅向，给出了⼀个反直觉的排序：⽹络安全。
他持仓中的 Okta 是典型案例。这家做⾝份安全的公司，核⼼业务是为⼈类提供⾝份认证，⼏年
前⾼增⻓，之后增速回落到个位数，"AI来了之后，2023到2025年这块核⼼业务并没有加速"。
转折发⽣在今年。

他给了⼀个量级对⽐：地球上有80亿⼈，⽽机器⾝份的数量最终可能是"上千亿"。
第⼆条线索是数据层。Kim ⽤⼀句话概括了他眼中未来企业的形态：
"模型能⼒⼤概⼀年提升⼀个数量级。如果算⼗年，就是⼀万倍；再⼗年，⼜是⼀万倍。这是⾮
线性的。"
"模型能⼒的增⻓速度，快于它在现实经济中的扩散速度。"
"这次Anthropic的上市，第⼀次出现⼀家纯粹的应⽤公司，也许它会开启AI的下⼀个阶段，让我
们⾛得⽐算⼒更宽。"
"现在有了Agent，它们不是⼈，是机器。今年出了这么多安全事件，从Hugging Face那次攻击
开始，⼤家意识到这些Agent可能会失控。所以你需要的不只是⼈类⾝份的安全，还有机器⾝
份、Agent⾝份的安全。"
"这个变化今年才刚刚开始，还处在极早期的阶段。"
"你会接⼊智能，选⼀个模型；中间是彭博、⻉莱德这样的数据层；上⾯跑着Agent。未来的公司
就⻓这样，Agent和你的数据交互，你往⾥灌原始智能。"

### Page 3

沿着这个图景，⻉莱德在数据管道、Agent构建、Agent防护三个环节上布了仓。他把这称作"企
业整体重构的第⼀局"。
99%的流量将由机器产⽣，⽽市场还在赌算⼒⻅顶
Kim 引⽤了 Cloudflare 的数据：今年年初，AI与机器流量已经超过⼈类流量。
他的推演是：每个⼈未来会有⼗个、甚⾄上百个Agent昼夜不停地⼯作。⼈类的刷⼿机流量在总
量⾥会变得微不⾜道，⽽更耗算⼒的是那些⾮⼈类的负载——药物研发、材料发现、化学与⽣
物学、机器⼈系统训练，"可能会有⼀整个数据中⼼只做药物发现"。
这正好回到他对市场定价的批评：
⾄于AI交易的新阶段怎么布局，Kim 的判断是软件、服务与AI模型将三合⼀，"以结果的形式交
付"。
他以⾃⼰的⼯作流举例：⽇历、邮件接⼊AI助⼿，每天处理两千封邮件、按优先级分流，再分设
多个Agent盯半导体、数据中⼼、基因组学，每周⽣成⼀份"AI在⽣物学领域的进展"⻓报告。

"如果今天机器和⼈类流量是六⽐四，五年后会是99⽐1。"
"越来越多的算⼒负载不是由⼈产⽣的，事实上99%都将是⾮⼈类的。"
"市场在说算⼒会在2028年⻅顶并下滑。这与即将到来的东西完全相反——所需的算⼒会⽐我们
现在设想的还要⾼出好⼏个数量级。这就是最⼤的分歧所在。"
"把AI模型、软件、服务这三样合成⼀个东西，以结果交付。然后把这个想法套⽤到标普的每⼀
个⾏业上。"
"现在还做不到让它替我把税报了，但⽅向是这样。"

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

Document 294: Capital Market Outlook (2026-11-23)

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
AI Implies Dramatic Shift in the Structure of the U.S. Economy
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
On November 16, Federal Reserve Chairman Kevin Warsh moderated three conference
sessions at a symposium focused on how AI will ripple through the economy. The event
was the first public look at one of the five task forces Warsh created to review the
Federal Reserve (Fed) processes for doing its job. The task forces are meant to help

reform the Fed by building a consensus for overhaul to address shortcomings Mr. Warsh
has raised about the Fed's performance over the years. The outsized role of AI in the
evolution of the U.S. economy is reflected in the task force to consider AI's impact on
employment and productivity. This is, perhaps, the least controversial of the various
issues the task forces are charged with considering but nevertheless one of major
importance.
The main takeaway from the symposium was that various scenarios remain possible

with quite different implications for the future of U.S. growth, employment and interest
rates. The wide range of outcomes has contributed to the recent volatility in equity
markets, especially the parts of the market most closely associated with the buildout
of AI data centers. Estimating the payoff from the buildout naturally remains a key
focus for investors but one that is subject to uncertainty because of the rapid pace of
technological innovation and the political pressures that are arising from public concerns

over the changes AI is bringing.
Exhibit 1 helps shed light on the dramatic consequences AI is likely to create for the
structure of the U.S. economy and employment. The cost structure of corporate output
as of 2025 includes about half of each dollar for labor. An estimate for the cost structure
of a dollar spent on inferential AI after the data center buildout reaches a net stock
centered around $5 trillion in 2030 requires only a fourth as much labor per unit of

revenue or output. The bigger the share of overall value inferential AI becomes in gross
domestic product (GDP) the lower the labor share of income will become. This is not a
new trend. The labor share of income has been gradually declining ever since the digital
economy began to develop over a half century ago. What is new is the speed of the
transformation. Self-generative AI is representative of the singularity, the point when the
spread of this digital transformation becomes exponential.

The drastically lower labor share of value implied by AI output implies that other costs
comprise bigger shares. For example, the exhibit shows the profit share of revenue is
much higher (28% vs.19%) for AI-generated output. This is already evident in the profit
profile of the S&P 500 where the margins and profit growth of the AI-related companies
are orders of magnitude greater than the profit profiles of non-AI-related businesses.
Presumably, as the old economy increasingly transforms to this new economy cost

structure, the productivity benefits of AI will increasingly transfer over to traditional
businesses and transform their cost structures in the direction of those of inferential AI
builders. This analysis suggests profit growth is moving toward a bigger share of output
over the next few years.
Competition naturally eats away at "excess" profits, those not justified by costs. Exhibit 1
partly illustrates the justification for higher AI profits. AI is capital-intensive compared

to the non-AI economy. This can be seen in the much higher depreciation-cost share
in the AI economy (28% vs.15%). AI capital is more short-lived than average, reflecting
the quicker obsolescence that naturally results from accelerating technological change
and less durable infrastructure (short-lived chips). This issue of rapid depreciation has
surfaced in the debate over potential AI profitability, with analysts trying to figure the
right life span of data centers to model their profit outlook.

Investment Implications
AI's creative destruction is likely to
leave a lot of laggards in its wake while
creating a stronger, more profitable
economy for investors. As a result,
we believe a diversified investment
approach favoring high-quality balance
sheets and reliable free cash flows is
warranted.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Finally, "other costs" make up about a third of AI output cost compared to just 12% for
non-AI output. Among these are the increased power costs associated with intensive
compute processing. Electricity costs are going to replace a lot of labor costs. As the
midterms made clear, rules and regulation for data centers, including power and water
issues, are likely to come under much greater scrutiny as well as other environmental and

"quality-of-life" issues.
Ultimately, this new cost structure for the AI economy will be the focus of innovation
to bring down costs and remove bottlenecks. Technology is ultimately deflationary.
Monetary policy is the cause of inflation. This "deflationary" or declining cost aspect
of technological progress is a key factor behind the notion that the promise of the
singularity is a world of superabundance. Intelligence, power and compute costs are likely

to become cheaper and cheaper as innovators work to create this superabundance.
Exhibit 1: Comparison of Corporate Cost Structure to Produce 2025 Output to
Corporate Cost Structure to Produce Inference Compute Revenue from AI Data
Center Buildout by 2030.

Employment and AI. The dramatic reduction in labor needed to run an AI cost structure
promises a much higher level of productivity and therefore higher levels of wealth and
income. As the cost structure in Exhibit 1 illustrates, however, a much bigger share
of output goes to capital and therefore shareholders in an AI-dominated economic
structure. There are various ways this change could sort itself out. In a society with an
increasingly older population, more capital income solves the problem of more retirees

needing to live on investment income. AI also promises to increase longevity. Therefore,
the future is likely to include more elderly people living on capital income as well as
more elderly people working. In addition, the bounty from higher productivity is likely
to support demand for new goods and services as past technology breakthroughs have
shown. People will be required to produce those new goods and services.
Nevertheless, it's clear from the distribution of capital income that's becoming more, not

less, concentrated, that policies that spread wealth ownership across a broader swath
of the population could potentially help ease the social tensions plaguing the population
under the guise of "affordability." The proliferation of gifts from the wealthy to Trump
Accounts is a reflection of the recognition that this issue needs to be addressed. The
U.S. Treasury rolled out temporary regulations at the end of September that allow Trump
Accounts to hold donated individual stocks instead of just diversified index funds. There

is likely to be a push to expand the growth of large philanthropic donations from the
private sector to fund retirement-type accounts for the broader population. This is an
alternative to higher tax rates that fund government spending rather than long-term
saving. A society that shares more broadly in the fruits of AI is more likely to tolerate AI's
profound changes.

### Page 4

4 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Too Connected to Fail: When the Jetsons Meet the Flintstones
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
This month, when a leading U.S. technology company went dark, planes across America
were grounded, and major banks went offline, investors learned an unsettling lesson on
the fragility of America’s digital economy.  We learned that interconnectivity is both a

strength and a weakness—and that the digital economy of the future rests upon the
shaky infrastructure of the past.
We live in a world where AI is advancing at warp speed. Computing power is expanding
exponentially. Financial transactions move around the globe in milliseconds. And cloud
computing keeps billions of people and businesses connected every day. It is a world
where everything is faster, digital and connected.
The U.S. digital economy now accounts for roughly 12% of GDP, expanding about three

times faster than the economy overall this century. Digital gross output totaled $3.7
trillion in 2021, the last year of available data from the Commerce Department; that said,
the figure almost certainly understates the current size of the U.S. digital economy. Digital
commerce now accounts for nearly 18% of all retail sales ($350 billion), although again,
this number is understated. Add in business-to-business digital transactions and digitally

delivered professional services, and the amount of daily digital commerce in the U.S. is
massive and growing robustly. Each one of these transactions ultimately depends on
communications services, electricity, computing facilities, and digital payments networks.
Yet here’s the rub: our digital world is juxtaposed against a physical infrastructure
of aging radar systems, antiquated telecommunications equipment, half-century-old
transmission lines, vulnerable underwater subsea cables and decades-old software.

Here we are, well into the 21st century, and a handful of Federal Aviation Administration
facilities are still using floppy disks to support aviation travel. Our world is a world where
the space-age Jetsons have collided with the Stone Age Flintstones.
Something had to give, and it did this November: business communications were
disrupted, planes were grounded, ATMs were frozen and investors were left wondering
“what’s next”?
In our opinion, “what’s next” entails a renewed focus and commitment on the part of the

U.S. public and private sectors to build America’s physical infrastructure back better and
faster. The recent disruptions, we believe, are a reminder that a nation’s infrastructure is
essentially its backbone, and every nation, like every human being, is only as strong as its
skeletal framework.
A healthy backbone allows an economy to move, pivot, grow, innovate and compete. An
impaired spine, in contrast, constrains mobility, undermines nimbleness and flexibility and

is prone to setbacks.
So, think of roads, bridges, ports, airports, electrical grids, communication networks and,
increasingly, digital networks not merely as conveniences of modern life. Rather, think
of them as foundational components of economic activity, national security and global
competitiveness. Without them, even the world’s most sophisticated economy can be
brought to a standstill.
The great irony—and potential opportunity—of the “too connected to fail” is that while the

U.S. leads the world in AI, cloud computing, and other advanced technological capabilities,
the physical infrastructure of the nation continues to show its age. Electrical grids are
struggling to accommodate increasing demand. Transmission networks are plagued with
bottlenecks. Aviation communications systems built in the 1970s are overwhelmed.
None of the above is new—America’s infrastructure deficiencies have been widely
acknowledged but frequently tolerated for decades. The grid held up most of the time,

while airline safety remained relatively stellar. Power outages during storms and heat
waves were expected and tolerated—no matter the misery created. All good.
Investment Implications
The future is digital, which
requires more capital investment
on America’s aging physical
infrastructure. We continue to favor
Industrials, infrastructure-related
firms, and commodities for the long-
run.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Today, however, it’s different. Because there is so much riding on our digital
connectivity—think trillions of dollars of daily commerce—the cost of failure or
disruption continues to soar. Case in point: America’s recent aviation meltdown. The
communications malfunction triggered a national four-hour ground stop, resulting in
nearly 20,000 flight cancellations over four days, at a potential cost to the airlines

of more than $1 billion in profits. Adding more salt to the wound, fried and frizzled
passengers absorbed an estimated $1.9 billion in additional economic costs. All because
the communications infrastructure supporting the world’s most sophisticated aviation
system failed to handle a technical upgrade.
Where are the Jetsons when you need them? We believe the recent technological failures
will accelerate infrastructure spending for a number of reasons. One, as shown above,

the economic costs of digital failures are rising. Two, America’s digital economy is now
too big—and too connected—to fail. Three, the extraordinary power needed to run
the AI revolution makes it absolutely essential that the U.S. stop talking about fixing
its aging infrastructure and start doing it. Four, with the stakes so large for America’s
largest technology companies, with enormous incentives of their own, these firms are
contributing their private capital of their own to build back America’s infrastructure

faster and better. And fifth, an aging air traffic control system, an unreliable national
grid, and weathered transmission lines―these are more than just economic and financial
challenges. They are challenges to America’s national security—notably to the great
power rivalry between the U.S. and China. This rivalry, in our opinion, will help create
more bipartisan support for increased infrastructure spending and greater public-private
sector cooperation.

Investment summary. It’s taken a series of technological snafus, but here we are—
America’s infrastructure is gaining the attention—and capital—it needs. It’s also
garnered the attention of Congress. Just last week, the Bipartisan American Affordability
Act, which reduces permitting burdens on a number of projects, passed a key procedural
vote in the Senate. Whether the House votes in favor of the bill remains to be seen but
in general, the U.S.’s cumbersome permitting and regulatory systems are coming under

more scrutiny.
To this point, the bipartisan National Governors Association recently outlined
recommendations designed to cut through the permitting and regulatory red tape. The
Department of Energy has developed a coordinated federal transmission permitting
framework designed to clear and approve projects at a faster pace. Yes, it will take
some time to cut through the labyrinth of federal, state and local approvals, permits, and
regulations. But the need for reforms has become quite evident and urgent, with political

inaction less acceptable to an electorate increasingly worried and wary of the next
technology glitch.
So, who potentially benefits in our theme of “too connected to fail”?  In our opinion,
the winners will include electrical equipment manufacturers, engineering firms, utilities,
telecommunications infrastructure providers, industrial automation companies, and
specialized construction firms. Specialists in maritime surveillance, underwater sonar
detection, and fiber-optics sensing capabilities are well positioned in this new world.

In terms of asset allocation, this means considering more exposure to infrastructure-
related industrial companies and leaders in renewables (solar, wind, electric vehicles,
biomass) and the required infrastructure behind each renewable energy source. Leaders
in electricity distribution, charging stations, batteries, as well as low-carbon hydrogen,
biomethane, and advanced biofuels should also be considered in portfolios. Ditto for
leaders in low-carbon technologies (LED lighting, smart energy meters, and storage),

and leaders in transmission technologies like high-voltage direct current (HVDC), which
transfers wind and solar power from where it is created to where it is needed.  We also
still favor commodities over the long term, as well as cybersecurity leaders.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
In our view, what does the market want and need to see to establish a firmer base?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
IInvestors are looking to reprice uncertainty for 2027. A lot has been digested by this
market since semiconductor stocks peaked in June. Inflation, Fed policy, geopolitics,
the funding of the AI buildout, CEO changes and future power adjustments out of the

midterm elections all in a matter of months.
Most importantly, investors kept clinging to the base—earnings and profitability. As long
as the base remained strong and growing, investors were willing to take the risk of a
market led by technology and the AI buildout more specifically.
Recently, investors have shifted to wanting more certainty: certainty around higher-
quality, transparency in FCF, the ability to secure financing if needed, reasonable

valuations and strong moats that last beyond peak profitability. This is why we have
experienced a shift from the more highly cyclical semiconductors to the hyperscalers in
terms of positioning.
The market wants to see that AI spending remains robust but with more reasonable
expectations for 2027 and perhaps 2028. A reset of expectations by the leaders of
the buildout can go a long way to finalizing this “flush” stage, in our view. We remain
diversified Equity and are proud Bulls overall.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading
companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is the equal-weight version of the widely-used S&P 500, in which each constituent company is given the same weight (approximately 0.2% each), rather than
weighting by market capitalization.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

### Page 1

COP31 Takeaways: Collapse or reset?
Climate diplomacy has stalled. COP31 in Antalya closed without a headline agreement, something that has not happened
since 2009. Talks on moving away from fossil fuels proved too divisive, and progress on funding for developing countries
to adapt was limited. Yet we see a necessary reset rather than a collapse. Electrification, a key step in the energy
transition, has support, and coalitions of willing countries may press ahead without waiting for the consensus on climate

Deutsche Bank
Research
Debbie Jones | Andrew Dixon
S U S T A I N A B I L I T Y  |  T H E M A T I C  R E S E A R C H  |  N O V E M B E R  2 0 2 6
10 key takeaways from COP31 (see pages 2–5 for a summary)

### Page 2

Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Takeaways  |  November 2026
COP 3 1  TAK EAW AYS   |   I NTR OD U CTI ON AND  OV ER V I EW
A collapse or reset? Why did COP31 talks break down and what happens next
2
The COP process stalled this year, but a reset may be on the horizon
The UN climate summit in Antalya (Türkiye) ended on Sunday without a headline
agreement, after a draft was rejected as too weak on fossil fuels. Many are calling
the outcome a collapse of the COP process, which brings together almost 200

nations to agree shared climate action. At the same time, the science now suggests
the world will overshoot the 1.5°C global warming limit set in the Paris Agreement,
and the pressure for tougher action is growing. The outcome is a headwind to
climate diplomacy, but it also suggests to us that elements of the COP process need
to evolve to ensure compatibility with the current economic and political backdrop.
A growing bloc is no longer willing to accept weak / no language on fossil fuels

The headline agreement or “final text” is made by consensus. While this is open to a
degree of interpretation, what is clear is that a few objections can block a deal. The
EU, Norway and several vulnerable countries did just that, arguing the draft fell short
of what is needed to tackle climate change. It was the first time since 2009 that a
COP has ended without an overarching agreement. More than 130 countries backed
stronger wording on fossil fuels, up from about 80 at COP30 last year. Resistance to

the language also grew, with some oil-producing countries pushing instead for a
bigger role for carbon capture technology, which widened the divide.
Poorer countries were left disappointed on climate finance commitments
Developing countries had hoped for clarity on last year’s agreement to triple funding
for adaptation finance. Strained public finances in developed countries made
progress more challenging. The US withdrawal from the Paris Agreement, together

with cuts to its overseas climate funding, has left a gap others are struggling to fill.
Private capital will be needed to fill the gap, but the economics must work
Public money alone will not deliver the $300bn a year that developed countries
agreed at COP29 to mobilise by 2035. Having argued the draft was too weak to
address climate change, Europe will be expected to help mobilise private capital at
scale. That means policy support and steps to reduce investors' risk, so that returns

are attractive enough to draw capital in greater numbers.
Voluntary action will also need to set a new standard for progress
We expect groups of like-minded countries and voluntary plans, which will be
intertwined with COP discussions without being dependent on the formal consensus
process, to increasingly drive progress on divisive topics. Examples include several
country specific Santa Marta coalition national plans to move away from fossil fuels

and Brazil's voluntary transition roadmap. In this respect, failure to reach consensus
at COP31 may prompt the change of pace needed for countries to move forward.
Electrification offers common ground
Türkiye's voluntary “35-by-35” pledge, which aims to lift electricity's share of global
final energy use from c.23% to 35% by 2035, was the clearest common ground in
Antalya. More than 80 countries endorsed it, including China and the EU, and it drew

strong business backing. Critics note it does not explicitly require clean power, but it
encourages it, and that flexibility helped widen support while fitting energy-security
goals. We expect focus on electrification at future COPs, with attention shifting from
endorsements to delivery, such as investment in grids and the electrification of
transport and industry, and to whether more countries can be brought on board.
What happens next?
Bonn’s mid-year talks will test whether negotiators can rebuild consensus on the

divisive issues. COP32 in Addis Ababa in 2027 will then turn to delivery, with a focus
on adaptation finance (a focus for many African nations). COP33 in 2028, which
concludes the second global stocktake and thus shapes countries’ next climate plans
due in 2030, still has no host: China reportedly declined to bid and India withdrew its
offer. A swift decision matters beyond logistics. COP31 may have faltered partly
because Türkiye and Australia had less than a year to prepare, which didn’t provide

enough time to build the necessary consensus.
This report is constructed in two parts. The first (pages 2-5) sets out our top 10 takeaways
from COP31 in short form, for readers who want our high-level thoughts and observations.
The second covers each takeaway in full, followed by a look at COP next steps, and an
appendix explaining key COP terms mentioned in this note.

### Page 3

Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Takeaways  |  November 2026
COP 3 1  TAK EAW AYS  |  TOP  1 0  (1  o f 3 )
Antalya - 10 takeaways from COP31
3
01
02
03
NO HEADLINE AGREEMENT
Consensus agreement tested; the COP
process will be forced to evolve
See page 7
– Amid questions over the effectiveness of multilateralism, COP31 delegates failed to agree on a headline agreement (i.e.,
the final cover text). This is important as the text is typically seen as a key signal for the global climate agenda. While this

outcome is a setback for the COP goal of collective climate progress, the result will force a rethink of the process.
– This result is disappointing for climate advocates and developing nations, but the outcome is also acknowledgement that
the current process is not delivering at the pace required to address the now anticipated temperature overshoot. We still
see a role for consensus decision making, but it now won’t be relied upon. Instead, we expect to see greater focus on

voluntary action and “coalitions of the willing”, whereby like-minded countries seek action on divisive issues.
MORE SUPPORT FOR FOSSIL FUEL
TRANSITION MET WITH RESISTANCE
Coalitions of the willing are emerging
and building transition roadmaps
See page 8
– Despite the lack of agreement on the final cover text, we took away from COP that there is a notable faction of support
for a transition away from fossil fuels. This is something to watch. Reports suggest that more than 130 countries backed

or would not object to stronger fossil fuel language, up from more than 80 at COP30. Energy security worries and
heightened concern over rising temperatures likely played a role in this shift.
– Away from the formal negotiations, Brazil’s COP30 presidency presented its voluntary global roadmap for transitioning
away from fossil fuels. While not binding, notable countries including India and Nigeria are reportedly welcoming it.
France, Germany, Finland, Denmark and the Netherlands have also published national transition roadmaps. More could

follow ahead of the second voluntary Santa Marta coalition meeting next July, co-hosted by Tuvalu and Ireland. This will
be an early test of whether high-level support translates into action, policy and investment.
STRAINED CLIMATE FINANCE
Developing countries are frustrated
with the COP process, while developed
countries are constrained
See pages 9–10
– Climate finance, including adaptation, was the priority for most developing countries at COP31, but progress was limited.

A key ask was clearer language defining the pledge to triple adaptation finance by 2035, agreed last year in Belém
without a headline figure or baseline year. An early call to solidify this as a $120bn annual target was dropped from later
drafts, though this was always the most likely outcome given the EU had ruled out new pledges.
– The shortfall in climate finance delivery against existing pledges is most concerning to the Global South, where climate

impacts are most severe and the capacity to fund adaptation is weakest. Developing countries often can’t meet their
Nationally Determined Contribution (NDC) goals to lower emissions without better access to finance, and this extends
beyond the amount available, with accessibility and conditions (particularly around debt burdens) a barrier.
– There was a breakthrough on technical aspects around refining a set of 59 indicators to measure adaptation progress

under the Global Goal on Adaptation. Once fully detailed, these indicators will provide important signals as to the areas
where finance for adaptation projects is most urgently required.

### Page 4

Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Takeaways  |  November 2026
Antalya - 10 takeaways from COP31
4
06
COP 3 1  TAK EAW AYS  |  TOP  1 0  (2  o f 3 )
07
05
IPCC TIMELINE IN QUESTION
No agreed timeline for the reports key
to the 2028 global stocktake
See page 12
– A recurring concern among attendees was a potential delay to the IPCC’s next assessment reports, for two reasons. First,
the reports are key to informing the second global stocktake, concluding in 2028. Second, our discussions suggest that

climate deterioration is happening faster than initially expected, thus delay risks decisions resting on out-of-date science.
– Even if there is agreement to produce the IPCC report on time, it may not be fully accurate, as the timeline may limit
contributions from scientists in developing countries, such as Kenya, who are struggling to secure the funding and
capacity needed to contribute within the shorter timeframe.
TRADE TENSIONS CONTINUE
Pushback against the EU’s CBAM and

other unilateral trade measures
See page 13
– Developing nations, including China, India and the African Group, pushed back against unilateral climate-related trade
measures, with the EU’s Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism (CBAM) a particular target. We expect trade to remain a
source of friction in climate diplomacy, while cleaner producers should gain a global trade advantage.
ELECTRIFICATION
The clearest common ground, with 80+
countries backing 35-by-35

See page 14
– Electrification was the clearest area of common ground in Antalya. The voluntary “35-by-35” pledge aims to raise
electricity’s share of global final energy use from about 23% today to 35% by 2035, and was endorsed by more than 80
countries, including China and the EU, with strong business support. Current and stated policies point to only 28–29% by
2035, so a gap remains.
– While aligned with broader energy security goals, the electrification pledge drew criticism for not requiring clean energy.

We viewed the ambiguity as a way to facilitate broader agreement and grow the infrastructure needed for a more
meaningful shift away from fossil fuels. We expect continued building on this goal at future COP events.
PRESSURE ON EUROPE
Having rejected the cover text, Europe
will be expected to lead action
See page 11
04
– Exiting COP31, we expect more diplomatic pressure on the European nations to back up their stance that the COP31
draft text did not go far enough to address climate change. The EU, UK, Norway and Switzerland all either formally

rejected or voiced opposition to the draft text. Countries choosing to walk away will be expected to show how they can
deliver the ambition they say the text lacked.
– Public finance constraints (a point of contention and a limiting factor for ambition at this year’s COP) will mean taking
steps to enable private capital, as well as driving the regulation needed to mobilise it. At issue is that investors face
limited transparency, hard-to-assess risk and a high cost of capital, which guarantees and other risk-sharing tools could

help reduce. As one participant noted, private finance is being asked to show up at COP. They need a reason to do so.

### Page 5

Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Takeaways  |  November 2026
5
Antalya - 10 takeaways from COP31
09
10
08
COP 3 1  TAK EAW AYS  |  TOP  1 0  (3  o f 3 )
AI & CLIMATE
A missed opportunity
See page 15
– AI was a missed opportunity. While the power demands of data centres were acknowledged as a driver of increased
energy demand, discussion of AI’s role in advancing climate goals was surprisingly limited: just 2% of the more than 4,000
Blue Zone events we analysed had a notable focus on AI. Given the strong presence of climate technology at COP, we had

expected AI to feature far more prominently.
– The Antalya AI Pledge drew a muted response with only seven governments among c.80 backers. Major US AI firms
stayed away, though Alibaba signed up. Launched by the Presidency with the ITU, it serves as a voluntary framework
committing signatories to measurable action to use AI for climate goals while curbing its own energy and resource
footprint. As the focus on data centres increases, we expect this topic to feature more prominently at COP.

US WITHDRAWAL HAD AN IMPACT, BUT
DEMOCRATS SHOW UP
The US exit has left a clear dent, but
Democratic governors express
optimism
See pages 16-17
– The Trump administration sent no official delegation for a second year. After exiting the Paris Agreement, the US is set to
become the first country to leave the UNFCCC, the treaty that underpins global climate diplomacy. More than 120 US
state, local and business leaders attended COP, but as observers without a seat at the negotiating table. This absence

was felt across negotiating rooms, with observers highlighting the key role previously played by the US in bridging
divides. For the next few years at least, this dynamic is set to continue.
– The Democrats showed up with a message of optimism. The America Is All In coalition held several panels at COP,
highlighting that following the midterms, Democratic governors will lead states home to roughly three-quarters of the US
population, supporting the view that state-level policy will remain a key driver of US clean energy and industrial

investment. While we tend to agree, the magnitude of the benefit isn’t entirely clear.
NATURE, CARBON MARKETS & OCEANS
Future progress needed, but some
action was notable
See page 18
– Warming oceans and rising sea levels are a growing economic risk, damaging coastal infrastructure and fisheries and
raising insurance costs. COP31 delivered momentum but no negotiated ocean outcome. Funding was modest, including a
CAD$14mn commitment from Canada to the Ocean Risk and Resilience Action Alliance.

– Forest finance received a boost, with Canada pledging up to $392.5mn to the Tropical Forest Forever Facility (TFFF),
launched at COP30, taking total sponsor pledges to $8.3bn. The TFFF matters because it pays countries to keep tropical
forests standing, making protection more valuable than clearing. This voluntary effort was launched by Brazil at COP30.
– Carbon markets inched forward as attention turned to putting Article 6, the Paris Agreement’s rulebook for trading

emissions cuts between countries, into practice. The first version of the international Article 6.2 registry went live, and
Norway agreed to buy up to 12 MtCO₂e of credits from Indonesia. On 12 November, 14 governments signed the Antalya
Statement on Mobilising Private Finance for Climate Action, with a playbook of eight policy levers to grow corporate
demand for high-integrity credits. Corporates and investors still stress the need for robust credits that deliver the

promised emissions reductions and limit greenwashing risk.
